With these words, Obojanski winds up a long lecture that tends to prove there is no such thing as a God, and that the soul is but a function of the body. I smile at his fears, which (I assure him) are quite groundless: I am not in danger of any doubt whatever on things fundamental.
"I now see that I look upon you as a friend, and talk to you about everything. I forget that you are a woman—and as yet all but a little girl."
And here the electric bell rings; its tinkle announces nothing out of the common to me!
"Who has come so late?" I ask, trembling all over.
"Roslawski, very likely. … He arrived yesterday, and wrote that he would be here; but I was not expecting him any longer."
I hear the servant's steps in the anteroom, and the door as it opens. Obojanski leaves the room, and presently I recognize that voice—*his* voice! He is explaining the cause of his delay in coming.
"Have you anyone with you?" he asks, evidently averse to seeing strangers now.
"No, no; only Smilowicz and Miss Dernowicz, whom you know. … Come along."

===Chinese===
奥博扬斯基以这番话结束了一篇旨在证明上帝不存在、灵魂不过是身体之功能的长篇大论。我对他的担忧报以微笑，并向他保证那完全是多余的：我不会在根本性的问题上产生任何怀疑。
"我现在看出，我已把你当作朋友，什么都和你谈了。我忘了你是个女人——而且还几乎是个小姑娘。"
这时电铃响了；那铃声对我来说并无任何不寻常之处！
"这么晚了谁来了？"我全身颤抖地问道。
"很可能是罗斯拉夫斯基。……他昨天到了，写了封信说要过来；不过我已经不指望他会来了。"
我听见前厅传来仆人的脚步声，然后是开门的声音。奥博扬斯基离开了房间，接着我辨出了那个声音——他的声音！他正在解释迟到的原因。
"有别人在你那里吗？"他问，显然此刻不愿见陌生人。
"没有，没有；只有斯米沃维奇和您认识的德尔诺维奇小姐。……进来吧。"

===Original===
This time my self-control has quite forsaken me, and I feel my face on fire. … My first impulse is to jump up from my chair and welcome him; fortunately, I have not the strength to rise.
I keep silence, hanging down my head, so as to conceal the working of my features. Smilowicz says something to me, but I cannot make out what.
In comes Roslawski; I bow without looking him in the face; indeed, I scarce raise my head at all.
I am terribly afraid I shall do some unexpected thing. A wild unaccountable terror comes over me, such as one feels when about to faint. I clench my teeth, expectant.
After a while, my nervousness passes away, and I can hear myself asking him about his voyage, about England, about the sea; the calm indifference of my own voice is a surprise to me.
The first coherent thought which strikes me is—that I am a handsome woman: that I *must* be handsome. Roslawski is talking to Obojanski; it is a long time since they met, and they must be left to themselves a little while. I get up from my armchair and go towards Smilowicz, who stands silently by, looking at a new book on one of the shelves. Cool, majestic, with head erect and bright eyes shining serenely in the gaslight, I walk by, close to Roslawski. I see myself as from without, clad in a clinging black dress, wearing a great soft and quaintly designed autumn hat; with outlines that form a graceful silhouette, slow movements, picturesque in their indolence, the outcome of a superfluity of latent vital force, kept down and subdued by the will.

===Chinese===
这一次我的自制力完全丧失了，我感到自己的脸在燃烧。……我第一个冲动是跳起来迎接他；幸亏我连站起来的力气都没有。
我沉默着，低着头，以掩藏我面部表情的变化。斯米沃维奇对我说了些什么，但我没有听清。
罗斯拉夫斯基走了进来；我鞠了个躬，却没有正眼看他；确实，我几乎没怎么抬起过头。
我极度害怕自己会做出什么意想不到的事。一种莫名的、不可理喻的恐惧攫住了我，就像快要晕过去时的感觉。我咬紧牙关，屏息等待。
过了一阵，紧张感消退了，我听见自己在问他旅途的事，问英国，问大海；我声音中的那种平静而淡漠的语调令我自己也感到意外。
涌入我脑海的第一个完整的念头是——我是个漂亮的女人：我一定很美。罗斯拉夫斯基正在和奥博扬斯基说话；他们很久没见面了，得让他们单独待一会儿。我从扶手椅上站起来，走向斯米沃维奇——他正默默地站在一旁，看着书架上的一本新书。我神态冷静而高贵，昂着头，明亮的眼睛在煤气灯光下安详地发着光，从罗斯拉夫斯基身旁走过。我从外部看见自己：穿着一件贴身的黑裙，戴着一顶设计别致而宽大的秋季软帽；轮廓勾勒出优美的剪影，动作舒缓，那份慵懒自有其如画之美，是过剩的潜藏生命力被意志压制和驯服后的表现。

===Original===
For the first time now I cast my eyes upon him, and meet that cold, critical glance of his. No one but myself has ever hitherto been able to look at me in such wise.
I am standing by Smilowicz, and stoop down with a motion full of elegance and grace, to read the title of the book he is perusing. And all the time I know that the other's cold glance is fixed on me.
"You have changed very considerably during the vacation, Miss Dernowicz," Roslawski says to me, in an undertone audible in the quiet room.
"Have I?" This I say with a smile, raising my head.
"Yes, you seem taller now, and more like a 'grownup.' Last year there was still something of the schoolgirl in your appearance."
I protest, laughingly, and try to talk with Smilowicz. But instead of listening to him, I am thinking.
Roslawski is to my mind not so much a man as a mechanical power, something of a nature that is hostile and full of hatred; something dangerous; a mesmeric influence. This tall, well-dressed, well-informed gentleman in glasses is not to my mind a living man: rather a sort of abstract idea. At times I can scarce believe him to have any personal existence at all.
I have somehow the impression that I am standing upon a railway track, in a whirlwind of frozen snow. Above the howling of the blast, I hear the thunder of an approaching train; but I remain rooted to the spot, my eyes fixed upon the cold unfeeling glare from the lamps of the engine rushing on and going to crush me:—rooted there as in a dreadful nightmare, and unable to take my eyes away from those calm and ever-dazzling lights. There I stand, waiting, powerless, full of hostility yet of self-abasement.

===Chinese===
我第一次抬起眼看向他，迎上了他那冷峻而审视的目光。在此之前，除了我本人，从来没有人能用那样的目光看我。
我站在斯米沃维奇旁边，以一种充满优雅和从容的姿态弯下腰，去看他正在翻阅的那本书的书名。而与此同时，我知道另一个人冷峻的目光一直落在我身上。
"您在假期里变化很大呢，德尔诺维奇小姐，"罗斯拉夫斯基低声对我说，声音在安静的房间里清晰可闻。
"是吗？"我微笑着抬起头说。
"是的，您现在似乎高了些，更像一个'大人'了。去年您身上还带着几分女学生的气息。"
我笑着表示抗议，试图和斯米沃维奇交谈。但我没有听他说什么，心思早已飘到别处。
在我心目中，罗斯拉夫斯基与其说是一个人，不如说是一种机械性的力量，一种带有敌意的、充满仇恨的本性；某种危险的东西；一种催眠般的影响。这个高大、衣着考究、博学多识的绅士，在我看来不是一个活生生的人：更像某种抽象的概念。有时候我简直无法相信他是一个真实存在的个体。
不知怎的，我有种印象，仿佛自己正站在铁轨上，四周是冰冻的雪组成的旋风。在呼啸的风声之上，我听见一列火车隆隆驶来；但我却钉在原地，双眼紧盯着那正向我疾驰而来、要将我碾碎的车灯射出的冰冷无情的光芒——如同钉在一个可怕的噩梦中，无法将目光从那些平静而永远刺眼的灯光上移开。我站在那里，等待着，无力动弹，满怀敌意，却又充满自卑。

===Original===
Tea is brought, and the conversation becomes general. To the atmosphere that always reigns at Obojanski's, Roslawski now brings a newly imported stock of British iciness and rigidity. We all are sensible of the bonds of I know not what invisible etiquette, enveloping and wrapping us up like subtle, unbreakable cobwebs: we no longer venture to laugh out loud; everything is suppressed and stiff and grey.
"So then," he says, without for a second taking his eyes off me during the whole of our conversation, "so then, you can manage to look at everything in life as an object of observation and severe minute analysis?"
"Yes, I can. Predominance of the thinking over the emotional faculties is a characteristic of my brain."
"Don't you consider this a disadvantage to you? Such constant vigilance must deprive you of all directness in feeling."
"To some extent, yes. But this want of directness is fully compensated by the very process of observation and analysis, which are a source of intense pleasure to me. Besides, in the place of mere intensity of impression, I attain a far wider range; for my mind has the pleasure of perceiving and discriminating certain nice shades, which escape the notice of others."
A smile rises to Roslawski's lips, and I feel my soul freezing within me.

===Chinese===
茶端上来了，谈话变得大家参与。奥博扬斯基家一向笼罩的那种气氛，现在又被罗斯拉夫斯基从英国带来的冰冷与僵硬新添了一层。我们每个人都感受到了某种无形礼仪的束缚，它如同纤细而坚韧的蛛丝，将我们层层缠裹：我们不敢再放声大笑；一切都变得压抑、僵硬而灰暗。
"那么，"在整个谈话过程中，他的目光一秒也没有离开过我，"那么，您确实能够把生活中的一切都当作观察和严格细致分析的对象来对待？"
"是的，我能。思考能力对情感机能的主导，是我大脑的特征。"
"您不觉得这对您来说是一种缺憾吗？这种持续的警觉必然会使您丧失一切情感的直接性。"
"在某种程度上，是的。但这种直接性的缺失，完全可以由观察和分析的过程本身来补偿，这个过程给我带来强烈的快感。此外，它取代了单纯的感受强度，给我以更为广阔的境界；因为我的心智乐于察觉和辨别某些微妙的色调变化，而这些是别人注意不到的。"
罗斯拉夫斯基的唇边浮起一丝微笑，而我感到灵魂在我体内冻结了。

===Original===
And now, summer is dead and gone: withered with suffering and desire, the flame-red flower of Life has fallen to the ground. Now once more the infinite ice-plains are stretching all around me. Behold the sun quenched in the black sky, and the greenish Northern Lights rising above the horizon. And my ice-cold dreams, that had died, now come to life again. And see! that Soul of mine, which trampled my flowers beneath her feet, girds up her loins and goes forth into the snowy Infinite, priding herself upon her sorry triumph, and singing joyously her lofty and sublime hymn to Death!
Oh, how terrible it is, when the Soul is victorious! How terrible!
---
The weather has changed very suddenly; it is nearly as warm as in summer, and the leaves seem to have turned yellow with heat.
I am coming home from the office, alone and forsaken by all.
I am dreaming (like a dream indeed it is) of the boundless fields, the picturesque ridges, the dark forests and fragrant meadows of Klosow. I see the park, too, with its neatly-trimmed shrubberies and lofty trees; their bare trunks and leafy tops forming a canopy high overhead under the sky, and the foliage turning yellow or red in the sunny glare. The pond, too, do I see—so large that it may be called a lake—the pond, bleak and desolate in the moonless, starless night; that night, when I broke away from the magic spell of Life, and slew my own felicity with my own hands.

===Chinese===
如今，夏天已经死去了：那火红色的生命之花，在痛苦和渴望中枯萎凋零。无限的冰原再一次在我四周延展开来。看啊，太阳熄灭在黑色的天穹中，淡绿色的北极光在地平线上升起。而我那些曾经死去的冰冷的梦，此刻又复活了。看啊！那个将我的花朵践踏在脚下的灵魂，正束紧腰带，向白雪皑皑的无垠之境进发，为自己那可悲的胜利而自矜，并欢快地高唱着那崇高而庄严的死亡颂歌！
啊，当灵魂获胜之时，这是多么可怕啊！多么可怕！
---
天气骤然变化；几乎像夏天一样热了，树叶似乎在酷热中变黄了。
我正从办公室回家，孤身一人，被所有人遗弃。
我梦想着——这确实如同一场梦——克沃绍夫无边的田野、如画的山岗、幽暗的森林和芬芳的草地。我还看见那座公园，修剪整齐的灌木丛和参天大树；光秃的树干与葱郁的树冠在天空下搭成高高的穹顶，树叶在耀眼的阳光下变黄或变红。我还看见那座池塘——大得可以称为湖——在无月无星的黑夜中显得荒凉而凄寂；就是那个夜晚，我挣脱了生命的魔法蛊惑，亲手扼杀了自己的幸福。

===Original===
Before my eyes, people are walking along the avenues, strewn with dry dead leaves. The slightest breath of air brings down from the trees these tatters and strips, once a purple kingly mantle: but men go on, pitilessly trampling down the rustling leaves.
Now I am in a strange humour—a sort of Pantheistic mood. My Ego is multiplying, growing into countless gods, and penetrating the whole world, wherein there is no room for aught save Me. And, therefore, prodigious amazement takes hold of me, when I think how all these crowds of people can tread upon my golden autumnal leaves, or glance at me, because I have a noticeable face and a hat *à la diable m'enporte*. Can I think that they live? There is no life but mine only.
No, they have not life.
And there—an immense way off, on the farther shore of the Ocean of Infinity—there he stands, he, the only foe worthy of me: and he waits that I should go onward to meet him!
And I—I stand in fear. For a week I have not been at Obojanski's, where he goes pretty nearly every night.
When the thought comes to me of the splendid sorrel mount I had, and of Janusz whose lips were so sweet, I have a mind to burst out crying. But I shall not go back there, unless. … Oh, if I could help going back!

===Chinese===
人们在我眼前走在铺满枯死落叶的林荫道上。最轻微的风也会将这些残片碎条从树上吹落——它们曾是一件紫色的王者之袍：但人们继续前行，无情地践踏着沙沙作响的落叶。
此刻我有一种奇特的心境——一种泛神论般的自我膨胀。我的自我在无限增殖，化为无数个神灵，渗透整个世界，而这世界里除了我没有容纳任何他物的空间。因此，当我想到所有这些人竟能踩在我的金色秋叶上，或因为我有一张引人注目的脸和一顶"随它去吧"式的帽子而瞥我一眼时，一种巨大的惊异便攫住了我。我能认为他们活着吗？除了我自己的生命，再没有别的生命了。
不，他们没有生命。
而在那里——在极远的地方，在无垠之海的彼岸——他站在那里，他是唯一配得上我的敌人：他正等着我前去迎接他！
而我——我站在恐惧之中。一个星期了，我没有去过奥博扬斯基家——他几乎每晚都去那里。
每当我想起我那匹栗色骏马，想起雅努什那甜蜜的嘴唇，我就忍不住想大哭一场。但我不会回到那里去，除非……啊，要是我能不去就好了！

===Original===
I have an irresistible inclination to seek for types amongst people. I do not like things accidental, either without logical connection, or without connection with the special nature of a given mind. If it depended upon me, I would, like a scientist at work in his laboratory, remove from every character whatever is unnecessary and unessential, lest this should render its reactions with others too complicated and obscure. For example, I should like to make of Obojanski a sage of ancient Greece, and eliminate from him everything that disagreed with this type. Smilowicz should be a narrow-minded Socialist: as matters stand, he is too clever for his type, and most needlessly cleverer than Obojanski. Roslawski is almost perfect. I should only desire—and this, too, for purely personal motives—that he might look upon marriage from a less absolutely ideal standpoint.
What my own type is, I do not know. Very likely I have none; and this has troubled my mind for ever so many a year. I am unable to find anything general in myself, or to define my own nature in one word and make an abstraction of it. For that, I am far too complex.
My father was a bricklayer; and yet there is nothing vulgar in my face or postures or motions. I sweep my floor and clean my own shoes: yet my hands are as soft as velvet. During the whole of my childhood, I used either to go barefoot, or in cheap, clumsy boots; yet my feet are white and bear no mark that I ever went so. My work for the greater part of the day—the adding up of innumerable columns of figures—is such as might benumb most brains, and yet I am quite able to think keenly. Though I neither write poetry, nor sing, nor paint, I have a thoroughly artistic mind. My way of living borders on the penurious; yet I have all the epicurean instincts of those who live at another's expense. After all, I am (as I am perfectly well aware) nothing extraordinary; and yet, to be the little that I am, I have not undergone one twinge of conscience; in all that is Me, there is not one atom of harm done to anyone, and no one single tear of any being alive.

===Chinese===
我有一种不可抗拒的冲动，想在人们中间寻找典型。我不喜欢偶然的东西，无论它们缺乏逻辑联系，还是与某一特定心灵的本质毫无关联。如果由我来决定，我会像一个在实验室工作的科学家那样，从每个人物身上剔除一切不必要和非本质的东西，以免它们使他与他人的互动变得过于复杂和难以辨认。比如说，我想把奥博扬斯基塑造成一位古希腊的贤哲，从他身上消除一切与此典型不符的东西。斯米沃维奇则应该是个心胸狭窄的社会主义者：就现状而言，他对于自己的典型来说太聪明了，而且完全没有必要比奥博扬斯基更聪明。罗斯拉夫斯基几乎是完美的。我只是希望——而且纯粹出于个人动机——他能从一个不那么绝对理想主义的角度来看待婚姻。
我自己的典型是什么，我不知道。很可能我根本没有典型；这个问题困扰了我许多年。我无法在自己身上找到任何普遍性的东西，无法用一个词来界定我的本性并将其抽象化。因为论复杂，我远甚于此。
我的父亲是个砌砖工人；然而我的面容、姿态和动作中没有任何粗俗之处。我自己扫地、自己擦鞋：但我的双手柔软如天鹅绒。整个童年时代，我要么赤脚，要么穿便宜笨拙的靴子：但我的双脚白皙，看不出任何曾那样行走过的痕迹。我每天大部分时间的工作——把无数列数字加在一起——足以使大多数头脑变得迟钝，而我却依然能保持敏锐的思考。虽然我不写诗、不唱歌、不画画，却有一颗彻头彻尾的艺术心灵。我的生活方式近乎吝啬；然而我有着那种靠别人供养的人才有的全部享乐主义本能。说到底，我（我自己很清楚）并非什么非凡之物；然而，要成为我这微不足道的存在，我未曾有过一丝良心的不安；在我所是的一切之中，没有一丁点伤害过任何人，也没有任何生灵为我流过一滴眼泪。

===Original===
A postcard from Martha, with a "Decadent" figure of a woman, all covered over with microscopic handwriting.
> "Grandfather is dangerously ill. I have not had a wink of sleep for a week, and am almost lightheaded in consequence. Nervous energy alone has sustained me till now: I cannot answer for the morrow. I continually feel as if my brain were swelling, and would presently fly to pieces. I am tormented with the horrible uselessness of undeserved pain. I don't want to think what the end of all this is to be. I only know that something within me is giving way. Never yet has my spirit been so broken down: I am now paying the score of the Past, and with usurious interest besides. The autumn of life has come upon me, taking me unawares: nor is it relieved by any reminiscence of a springtime that never was mine. Every night, and all night long, I am sitting by poor Grandfather's bed, going over my interminable litany of sorrow, and shedding my heart's blood drop by drop.—M."
And about Janusz not one word!
---
As I am going home from the office today, I come across Smilowicz, with a big parcel of books under his arm. In spite of his ridiculous smile, the man impresses me: the life he leads is in such strict conformity with the doctrines which he professes. Obojanski tells me he is a very able teacher of Natural Science; but he loses all his lessons, because he cannot reconcile his advanced opinions with what the school superintendents require. For some time past, he has had nothing, or nearly nothing, to eat: he spends his mornings in the University Library, and his evenings at Obojanski's.
As we pass along by the "Philharmonia" building, he informs me that he has never been inside it.
"Do you object to going there?"
"Most certainly. I am against music, fine costumes, everything that represents satisfaction and amusement. To me all that only suggests extortion, wrongdoing, and injustice: for but a few are able to go there, and that only at the expense of others."

===Chinese===
一张玛莎寄来的明信片，上面印着一个"颓废派"风格的女人图案，背面密密麻麻写满了蝇头小字。
> "祖父病危。我已经一个星期没有合眼了，几乎快要晕眩。全凭一股神经在支撑着我：我不知道明天还能不能撑下去。我总感觉大脑在膨胀，随时会炸裂。这种毫无意义的、不该遭受的痛苦折磨得我死去活来。我不想揣测这一切的结局会是什么。我只知道内心的什么东西正在崩溃。我的精神从未如此被击溃过：我正在偿还过去的债，而且还带着高利贷的利息。生命的秋天出其不意地降临在我身上：而我对那从未属于我的春天，也无任何回忆可以聊以慰藉。每个夜晚，整夜整夜，我都坐在可怜的祖父床边，反复吟诵着那永远念不完的悲歌，一滴一滴地沥着心头的血。——玛。"
关于雅努什，竟只字未提！
---
今天我从办公室回家的路上遇到了斯米沃维奇，他腋下夹着一大捆书。尽管他那笑容可掬的样子令人发笑，这个人却给我留下了深刻印象：他过的生活与他所宣扬的学说严丝合缝。奥博扬斯基告诉我，他是一个非常出色的自然科学教师；但他丢掉了所有的课时，因为他无法将他那前卫的观点与学校督导们的要求调和。近一段时间以来，他几乎没有东西吃：上午在大学图书馆里度过，晚上则待在奥博扬斯基家。
我们路过"爱乐"大楼时，他告诉我他从未进去过。
"您不想去那里吗？"
"当然不想。我反对音乐、华丽的服饰、一切代表享乐和消遣的东西。在我眼中，那些都只意味着敲诈、恶行和不公：因为只有少数人能进去，而且是以牺牲他人为代价的。"

===Original===
"But you forget that wrongdoing and injustice are by no means essentials of the Beautiful, of Art, and of artistic delight, though at the present time they happen to exist in connection with these. Your theory seems to me to make far too much of what actually *is*. Try to deliver yourself from the fetters of the Temporal; look upon the present day, as being yourself outside of it and soaring above it: do you see what I mean?—I also resent whatever is unjust, but I can separate the Beautiful therefrom and love it, both in Art and in life."
"Well, you may be right, but I cannot take up so objective a point of view. In me indignation overbears any gentler artistic sentiment. Yet more: I think it is not now the time to enjoy Art, or to plunge into the deep and subtle analyses of Estheticism. What we want at the present time is Action."
"But, for myself, I quiet my conscience with the fact, which I know to be true, that I am living now just as I should live in that future when, as Ferri says, all are from the very outset to have equal opportunities for their development."
Smilowicz is pleased.
"Ah, then you understand. … I was afraid you had been surprised at my friendship for Obojanski, seeing the way I am accustomed to talk. But, you know, if scientific work were properly remunerated, Obojanski's monographs would bring him in enough money to live as sumptuously as he is doing now. So he arrives at the very same result, though by different roads. Yet that, unfortunately, is paltering with principles."
Oh, I should not object, so far as I am concerned, to any such "paltering!" As things stand, I am working too much: I might work less and do something better. … All my talent is quite thrown away on those everlasting accounts.
My dream is now, how to make more money. And this renders me somewhat uneasy; perhaps it is on account of pecuniary circumstances that I am now considering the possibility of marriage with Janusz, in case Roslawski. …
This I should not like. Not because it would show my character in an ignoble light. That's nonsense. No, but it would mark how little I care for the creature I could take on such terms.
---
I am of those whose sin is greater than the sin of Eve and Adam: I have eaten of the fruit of the knowledge that there is neither good nor evil.

===Chinese===
"可是您忘了，恶行和不公正绝非美、艺术和审美快感的必要组成部分，尽管它们此刻恰好与这些东西并存。您的理论在我看来过分强调了实际存在的东西。请尝试挣脱现世的镣铐吧；置身事外，超越当下来看待当今世界：您明白我的意思吗？——我也憎恨一切不公正之事，但我能将美从中分离出来，在艺术和生活中去爱它。"
"也许您说得对，但我无法采取如此客观的立场。在我身上，义愤总是压倒一切较为温和的艺术情感。更重要的是：我认为现在不是享受艺术或沉浸于美学之深邃精微分析的时候。我们现在需要的是行动。"
"但对我个人而言，我用一个我知道为事实的东西来使良心平静：那就是我现在的生活方式，正如在那个费里所说的、人人从一开始就拥有平等发展机会的未来中我应有的生活方式一样。"
斯米沃维奇很高兴。
"啊，那您就理解了。……我担心您看到我惯常的说话方式，会对我和奥博扬斯基的友谊感到诧异。但是，您知道，如果科学工作能得到应有的报酬，奥博扬斯基的专著会给他带来足够的金钱，让他过上和现在一样奢华的生活。所以结果是一样的，只是途径不同。不过，遗憾的是，那是在原则问题上含糊其辞。"
哦，就我个人而言，我不反对任何这样的"含糊其辞"！就目前的情况来看，我工作得太多了：我本可以少工作一些，做些更好的事。……我的全部才能都在那些没完没了的账目中白白浪费了。
我如今的梦想是，怎样赚更多的钱。这让我感到些许不安；也许正是因为经济上的考虑，我正在考虑嫁给雅努什的可能性，以防罗斯拉夫斯基那边……
这我可不愿意。不是因为这会把我品性中卑劣的一面暴露出来。那是胡说。不，而是因为这会表明，我对于一个在这种条件下可以接纳的人是多么地不在意。
---
我属于那种罪孽深于夏娃和亚当的人：我吃了那知善恶之果——知道世间既无善也无恶。

===Original===
Yes, for I have gone on—on to the very end. Everyone has something he can call his own. Sufferers magnify in their mind the power of suffering; those who have abandoned everything make a god of their strength of will to do so. But I have nothing left, nothing absolutely. Of beauty I have not enough to love that beauty in myself. Wisdom is wisdom from one standpoint only: that lost, its very idea ceases to exist. I have too much mind to be artful and mysterious, so I strike no one as being uncommon. I have all the shortcomings of a perfect sage; for I believe in nothing, and am indifferent to all things. But I am not, as sages are, encyclopaedic, nor do I love knowledge, nor have I any. At the same time, I do not, like a typical Decadent, hug myself at the thought of my doubts and of my indifference. Quite the contrary—to others, nay, even to myself, I play the role of one that is blithe, well-favoured, happy, and quite satisfied with being what I am. This is not because I deliberately try to keep my secret to myself, but because merriment is to my mind less wearisome than the apathy of doubt. And I have not as my own even what I say here, for I am not sure that it is true. …
Roslawski …? Well, say I am attracted by the interest of an experiment.
Out of which I am making a grave and important affair, simply because of my love for some pathos in life. …
---
Why, Janusz himself can be "distinguished" on certain occasions.
> Madame, (he writes),
> May I venture to remind you that the period chosen by you, within which to give me a definite answer, will have come to an end on Monday next?
> I beg to remain, Madame,
> Most respectfully yours,
>
> Janusz.
Yes, the time has come. I shall go to Obojanski's tonight.
---
Here I have come, with fevered lips and ice-cold heart, only to find that Roslawski went away but a quarter of an hour ago, having to dine with some friends this evening.
I still can smell in the air the brand of cigars that he smokes. … My eyebrows and lids are twitching as if agitated by some witch's spell.
Yet I experience not the least disappointment at not finding him here: rather a sense of relief, that I can put the affair off a little longer.

===Chinese===
是的，因为我已经走到了——走到了尽头。每个人都拥有某种可以称为自己之物。受苦者在心中放大了受苦的能力；抛弃一切的人则将自己那份意志力奉为神明。但我已经一无所有，什么都没有了。我的美不足以让我去爱自身之美。智慧只有一个立场上的智慧：失去了那个立场，智慧的概念本身也不复存在。我太过聪明而无法显得诡秘莫测，因此无人觉得我有什么不凡。我具有一个完美的贤哲的全部缺陷：因为我什么都不信，对一切都漠不关心。但我并不像贤哲那样博学多识，也不热爱知识，甚至没有任何知识。同时，我并不像典型的颓废派那样，一想到自己的怀疑和冷漠就暗自庆幸。恰恰相反——对他人，甚至对我自己，我都扮演着一个快乐、俊美、幸福且对自己的存在完全满足的角色。这并不是因为我刻意想保守秘密，而是因为在我看来，欢愉比怀疑的冷漠更少令人疲惫。而我甚至连这些话也不拥有，因为我并不确定它们是真实的。……
罗斯拉夫斯基……？好吧，就说我是被一场实验的兴趣所吸引吧。
而我把这当成了严肃而重大的事，仅仅是因为我生活中那份对某种悲情的偏爱。……
---
不过，雅努什自己在某些场合也能显得"体面"。
> 夫人（他写道），
> 可否冒昧提醒您，您所选定的给予我明确答复的期限，将于下周一届满？
> 敬请夫人接纳我最诚挚的敬意，
>
> 雅努什。
是的，时间到了。我今晚去奥博扬斯基家。
---
我来了，双唇发烫，心却冰冷，结果发现罗斯拉夫斯基一刻钟前刚走，他今晚要和一些朋友共进晚餐。
空气中还能闻到他抽的那种雪茄的气味。……我的眉毛和眼皮在抽搐，仿佛被某种巫术所搅动。
然而，没有在这里见到他，我一点也不失望：倒有一种解脱感，这件事可以再拖一拖了。

===Original===
Obojanski tells me what a favourite of Roslawski I am, and goes so far as to hint—in jest—that he is in love with me. This very evening he was asking why I have paid no visit to my old Professor for such a length of time. This, for a man of his sort, must mean a great deal.
In the main, however, Obojanski is this evening in a pessimistic and quarrelsome mood. He blames me for too readily taking up with new trends of thought: which does me great harm. There is no contemporary poet equal to Homer: I ought therefore to be somewhat more deeply read in the works of the old classics, which reflect such a healthy feeling of harmony between body and mind.
"You are," he says, "daily less mindful of the admirable maxim, *Mens sana in corpore sano*."
"Why, no; I decidedly uphold proper care of the body, to make it hardy and healthy, and able to resist the wear and tear of our now over-subtle and oversensitive minds."
"Yes, but nowadays our very minds are diseased."
"Well, then, let my motto be: 'In a sound body, a diseased mind!'"
Or what people may choose to call diseased.

===Chinese===
奥博扬斯基告诉我我是多么受罗斯拉夫斯基的青睐，甚至暗示——以玩笑的方式——他爱上了我。就在今天晚上，他还问我为什么这么久都不来看望我的老教授。对一个像他这样的人来说，这必定意味深长。
然而，奥博扬斯基今晚总的来说情绪悲观而好争论。他指责我太轻易地接受了新思潮：这对我大有害处。当代没有哪个诗人能与荷马相提并论：因此我应该更深入地研读古典作品，那些作品反映了身体与心灵之间何等健康的和谐感。
"你，"他说，"越来越不把那句箴言放在心上了：健全的心智寓于健全的身体。"
"不，并非如此；我坚定主张妥善照顾身体，使之强健、健康，能够承受我们如今过于精细和敏感的心灵之消耗。"
"可是如今我们的心灵本身就是病态的。"
"那么，就让我的格言是：'健康的身体里，住着一颗病态的心灵！'"
或者按人们愿意的说法，称之为病态。

===Original===
Scholars and thinkers, though they surely must have made some studies in logic, yet reason thus by analogy: Disease of the body is any departure from its normal state: consequently, any but an average mind is diseased. But if they start from the premise that mind and body are identical, then why reason at all on the matter?
Now I would burst into song, to run about in the open country, flooded with the white low sunbeams, and to utter cries of joy.
Why? Because the old Professor has said: "He is in love with you!" And because I believe he has spoken the truth.
Yes, and I shall continue to believe it till sundown, and I still am dreaming of a joyful triumph. I had one before, but it was far from joyful.
Tomorrow evening I shall go to see Obojanski once more; and I shall tremble with great fear.
---
I am sure my answer to Janusz will be delayed. Tonight Smilowicz and Roslawski accompanied me home together. I cannot say whether I shall get any opportunity for a private talk with him. Perhaps it is better so: but, then, Janusz is waiting for his answer down in Klosow.
Roslawski is the one man in the world before whose gaze my eyes must droop. That alone can throw me off my balance, rob me of my customary untroubled assurance; for it is the only force able to master mine.
Towards the end, our talk turns to love and marriage.

===Chinese===
学者和思想家们，虽然他们肯定研习过一些逻辑学，却如此类推推理：身体的疾病就是偏离其正常状态的任何状态；因此，任何偏离平均水平的心智都是病态的。但如果他们的前提是身心同一，那又何必就此事进行推理呢？
此刻我真想放声歌唱，奔向原野，沐浴在低垂的白色阳光下，发出欢乐的呼喊。
为什么？因为老教授说了："他爱上了您！"因为我相信他说的是实话。
是的，我将一直相信到日落时分，我还在梦想着一场欢乐的胜利。我曾有过一次胜利，但远谈不上欢乐。
明天晚上我要再去拜访奥博扬斯基；我会怀着巨大的恐惧颤抖。
---
我确信对雅努什的答复会推迟。今晚斯米沃维奇和罗斯拉夫斯基一起送我回家。我不知道是否有机会和他私下交谈。也许这样更好：不过，雅努什在克沃绍夫等着他的答复呢。
罗斯拉夫斯基是这世上唯一一个我不得不在他目光下垂首的男人。仅此一点就足以打乱我的平衡，夺走我惯常的无忧无虑的自信：因为它是唯一能驾驭我的力量。
临近尾声，我们的谈话转向了爱情和婚姻。

===Original===
The latter Smilowicz looks upon from an economic standpoint, and thinks it is, in our present conditions of life, a necessary evil. All the same, he informs Obojanski that a certain mutual acquaintance of theirs, who married not long ago, is perfectly happy with his wife.
"Ah, yes," Obojanski guardedly observes, "in the first months, even such a thing is not impossible."
Roslawski's face puts on a cold smile. Indeed, he is in favour of marriage, as is quite natural with a man who has sown his wild oats, and is desirous of love that is lawful. The fastest men I ever knew were theoretically in favour of monogamy. Imszanski, too, always told Martha that, were it not for the fickleness of women and various other untoward conditions, he would be happiest with one woman and one alone.
On this point, Obojanski is a sceptic; this is the only subject on which he can speak wittily.
"And you—do you intend to marry for love?" Roslawski asks me suddenly, with a subtle tinge of flippancy in his tone, such as men of his kind always use in speaking to women: an attitude with him quite instinctive and unreasoned, since he is very far from sharing Obojanski's prejudice concerning the inferiority of our sex.
A sudden qualm of terror seizes me, but I master it, and say with a tranquil smile: "Your question makes me feel as if under examination. Confess now that you are at present wanting to know what my reply will be, not what I really intend to do."
There is an ironical gleam in his eyes.
"You may take my word for it that I am not," he answers emphatically.
"In that case, I'll tell you as much as I myself know. If I marry for love, it will not last very long; if, on the contrary, I do so with judgment and out of a conscious conviction that the man is destined for me, then I shall be faithful to my husband all my life."
"And which of these alternatives do you prefer?"
"The second," I reply; and add truthfully, "for there are certain classes of feeling in which I object to changes."

===Chinese===
斯米沃维奇从经济的角度来看待婚姻，认为在我们当前的生活条件下，它是"一种不可避免的恶"。尽管如此，他告诉奥博扬斯基，他们的一位相识不久前结了婚，和妻子过得很幸福。
"啊，是的，"奥博扬斯基谨慎地评论道，"头几个月里，连这种事也不是不可能的。"
罗斯拉夫斯基脸上掠过一丝冷冷的微笑。他确实赞成婚姻，这对于一个已经放荡够了、渴望合法爱情的男人来说是很自然的。我所认识的最放纵的男人在理论上都赞成一夫一妻制。伊姆尚斯基也总是对玛莎说，如果不是因为女人的善变和种种其他不利条件，他和一个女人相守一生会是最幸福的。
在这个问题上，奥博扬斯基是个怀疑论者；这是他唯一能说得风趣的话题。
"而您呢——您打算为爱结婚吗？"罗斯拉夫斯基突然问我，语气中带着一种微妙的轻率，这类男人对女人说话时总不免如此：这对他来说完全是一种本能的、未经反思的态度，因为他远不像奥博扬斯基那样对女性的低劣抱有偏见。
一阵突如其来的恐惧使我一阵战栗，但我镇定下来，平静地微笑着答道："您的问题让我觉得自己在接受审问。坦白说吧，您现在想知道的是我的回答，而不是我真正的打算。"
他的眼中闪过一丝嘲讽的光芒。
"您完全可以相信我，我不是那个意思，"他断然答道。
"既然如此，我就把我自己所知道的告诉您。如果我为爱结婚，那爱不会持续太久；反之，如果我出于理性的判断，出于一种确信此人命中注定属于我的信念而结婚，那我将对丈夫忠贞一生。"
"您更倾向哪一种？"
"第二种，"我答道，并如实补充道，"因为有些类型的感情，我是不愿改变的。"

===Original===
"Really? But you would have the same result, even if you married for love."
"I am afraid I cannot bring myself to believe in the eternal duration of mere feeling. Love in marriage, as a rule, becomes in time a sort of mutuality of habit, a sense of solidarity, as it were, and now and then even a brotherhood of minds. It is just in such cases that divorce would be advisable."
"And when it is a marriage of reason?"
"Why, then the question is correctly stated from the first; at the outset, suitability of characters and of individualities are taken into consideration, so as to prevent any possibility of future disagreement."
"And yet it is possible to obtain the continuance of love by incessantly watching over it, by not unfrequently putting on a mask, and by keeping private certain emotions and states of mind which might prejudice one party in the eyes of the other."
How the remembrance of Janusz comes back to me as I listen! Of all this, *he* knew nothing at all.
"I doubt whether so much trouble is very profitable," I return. "The game is hardly worth the candle."
"And yet some there are," he goes on to say, "for whom present bliss has no value, if they know beforehand that the morrow will take it away. And they often prefer to renounce it entirely."
The words are spoken calmly, without any apparent significance; yet there is in their tone, I fancy, an undercurrent of ominous import.
"Well," I say, repressing my irrational dread, "then let all such take care to marry with judgment."
"Nevertheless, to give love and get in its place only intellectuality is not a good bargain, I fear."
Now—*now I understand*—and I almost feel hatred for the man. Yes, I may throw myself under the wheels of a locomotive, but never will I say I do that out of love for it!
"Reasonable people should remember that 'the heart is no servant,' and that, beyond intellectual and conscious resolve, we can find nothing on which we can safely count." This I say, as light-heartedly and as smilingly as I can, feeling meanwhile the dismay of a horrible misgiving—almost a certitude—clutching at my heart.

===Chinese===
"真的？但即使您为爱结婚，结果也是一样的。"
"恐怕我无法让自己相信纯粹的感情能够天长地久。婚姻中的爱情，久而久之往往会变成一种习惯性的相互依存，一种团结感，甚至不时还成为心灵的兄弟情谊。恰恰是在这种情况下，离婚才是上策。"
"那如果是一桩理智的婚姻呢？"
"那样的话，问题从一开始就摆对了位置；一开始就把性格和个性的契合度考虑在内，以防止未来出现任何分歧的可能。"
"然而，通过不懈的守护、不时戴上面具、以及隐瞒某些可能使一方在另一方眼中失分的情绪和心理状态，来维持爱情的延续，也是可能的。"
我听着，雅努什的记忆又涌上心头！对于这一切，*他*一无所知。
"我不认为费这么大心思有多大收益，"我答道，"这场游戏不值得花那么多蜡烛。"
"然而有些人，"他继续说，"如果事先知道明天就会失去今天的幸福，那今天的幸福对他们就毫无价值。他们往往宁可完全放弃它。"
这些话说得很平静，表面上看不出什么深意；然而我觉得，在那语气之下暗藏着某种不祥的含义。
"好吧，"我抑制住自己那不合理的恐惧，"那就让这些人都注意用理智来结婚吧。"
"然而，付出爱而换来的仅仅是理性，这笔买卖恐怕并不划算。"
现在——*现在我明白了*——我对这个人几乎生出了恨意。是的，我可以把自己投到机车的轮下，但我绝不会说我那样做是出于对它的爱！
"理性的人应该记住'心灵不是仆人'，在理智和自觉的决心之外，我们找不到任何可以赖以安心的东西。"我说着，尽可能显得轻松、面带微笑，而与此同时，一种可怕的疑虑——几乎是一种确信——正揪住我的心，让我惊恐不安。

===Original===
And now at last I am alone with Roslawski and Obojanski. I remain in my corner all the evening, saying little, overwhelmed with dread of the coming decisive moment. That tall, red-haired gentleman in glasses—I simply detest him!
Roslawski sets to playing Wagner—stiffly, correctly, like an automaton. His playing grates strongly upon my nerves: each of the notes taps on my heartstrings.
Obojanski is enchanted. He goes about the room on tiptoe, making the floor creak as he walks; he fetches music from the bookshelves for Roslawski, and lays them in heaps on the piano. Now and again he glances at me, and whispers, almost aloud: "How very beautiful!" He finally brings me a volume of some German encyclopaedia, and opens it at the article "Wagner," which he expects me to read.
I am so upset that I nearly break down. Resting my head on the back of the sofa, I look up at the ceiling to swallow down my tears as they well up. And I begin to weave fancies.
A wonderful immemorial forest, through which, clad in armour, knights are riding on white steeds. Most lofty oaks, strong-limbed and gnarled, with black trunks and dark-blue foliage, strike their roots deep into the ground. Amid mosses in hue like malachite, ferns put forth their sprays of sea-green lace. Fairies dance merrily among the trees, and scatter round them pearls of ringing laughter. And far away, lost in reverie, upon a dark, enchanted lake there floats a swan. A strange, clear, chilly splendour illuminates the twilight.
All at once a thunderbolt, a red thunderbolt falls: and the oak forest and the lake vanish into the depths of the earth. … Yet thunderstorms only take place on sultry summer days.
No, no, all this was but a dream.

===Chinese===
现在终于只剩下我和罗斯拉夫斯基以及奥博扬斯基了。我整晚都待在我的角落里，话不多，被即将到来的决定性时刻的恐惧压得喘不过气。那个戴眼镜的红发高个子绅士——我简直厌恶他！
罗斯拉夫斯基开始弹奏瓦格纳——生硬的、精准的，像一架自动机。他的演奏猛烈地刺激着我的神经：每一个音符都叩击着我的心弦。
奥博扬斯基如痴如醉。他踮着脚在房间里走来走去，地板在他脚下吱嘎作响；他从书架上给罗斯拉夫斯基取乐谱，堆在钢琴上。他不时地看我一眼，几乎大声地低语："多美啊！"他最后给我拿来一卷德国百科全书，翻到"瓦格纳"的条目，期望我去读。
我心烦意乱，几乎要崩溃了。我把头靠在沙发背上，仰望着天花板，强咽下涌上来的泪水。然后我开始编织幻想。
一座美妙的远古森林，身披铠甲的骑士骑着白马穿行其间。最高大的橡树，粗壮而虬曲，黑色的树干和深蓝色的枝叶，将根须深深扎入大地。在孔雀石色的苔藓间，蕨类植物舒展着海绿色的花边。精灵在树丛中欢快起舞，洒落一圈圈珍珠般清脆的笑声。而在远方，在一座幽暗而迷人的湖面上，一只天鹅在如梦似幻中漂浮。一种奇异、清澈而清冷的辉煌照亮了暮色。
忽然间，一道闪电，一道红色的闪电劈下：橡树林和湖泊都沉入了大地的深渊。……然而雷暴只在闷热的夏日才会发生。
不，不，这一切不过是一场梦。

===Original===
Now there comes before me the infinite wilderness of my own ice-plains, hard-frozen beneath the cold and glassy skies. I am afraid, I am horribly afraid, I cannot breathe, seeing those endless plains of ice, under that canopy of green and frosty light: it is the kingdom of my soul!
But far away, at the skyline, where without warmth the Aurora Borealis beams, there stands a huge statue, a basalt-hewn statue. This recks not of the unbounded wilderness, nor of the chilly gleams of the Northern Lights, nor of the stars, those silver eyes of Time. Tranquil and undismayed it stands. That is Roslawski.
On I march towards him, plodding through the deep and drifting snow; at his feet, I fall upon my knees.
And I beseech him to hide the boundless wilderness from my sight; to protect me from the icy air of death, so that I may dwell in this land of my soul, and yet not die. "For behold, this day I am weak exceedingly, this day I stand in fear of the plains of ice."
But he says: "Here in the snows around me, you must first lay out a garden as of the tropics; and yourself must blossom into a flame-red and purple rose."
And I make answer: "My lord, without the light of the sun, how is any rose to blow?"
Once more a thunderclap resounds. He is gone. I am all alone amid my ice-plains: and I live yet.
Bound I am, with fetters made of ice. The silvery wings of my soul are glittering under the canopy of heaven, in the greenish splendour of the Northern Lights. She would not share with me my years of burning heat, and now she will not have me share this realm of hers. A snake is lying on my bosom, and, coiled about my neck, sucks the warm blood thence. …
We bid good night to Obojanski, and go out into the street together.

===Chinese===
此刻，在我眼前展现的是我自己那冰原的无边荒原，在寒冷如玻璃的天空下冻结成坚硬的荒野。我害怕，我可怕地害怕，看着那些无尽的冰原，在那绿色而寒霜般的穹顶之下，我无法呼吸：那是我灵魂的王国！
但在远方，在地平线上，在没有温暖的北极光闪耀之处，矗立着一尊巨大的雕像，一尊由玄武岩雕凿而成的雕像。它不介意那无边的荒原，不介意北极光的冰冷辉光，也不介意那些星辰——时间的银色眼睛。它庄严而镇定地矗立着。那就是罗斯拉夫斯基。
我朝着他行进，在深陷的漂雪中艰难跋涉；到了他的脚下，我跪倒在地。
我恳求他将那无边的荒原从我的视线中遮蔽；保护我免受死亡的冰冷气息，让我可以居住在我灵魂的这片土地上，而又不至于死去。"因为看哪，今日我极其软弱，今日我站立在冰原的恐惧面前。"
但他说："在我四周的雪地中，你必须先开辟一座如同热带般的花园；你自己必须绽放成一朵火红与紫红交织的玫瑰。"
我答道："吾主，若无阳光照耀，玫瑰如何能开？"
又一声雷鸣轰然而至。他消失了。我独自一人置身于我的冰原之中：而我依然活着。
我被冰铸的锁链束缚着。我灵魂的银色羽翼在天穹下闪耀，在北极光的绿色辉煌中熠熠生光。她不愿与我分享燃烧的炽热岁月，现在她也不让我分享她的王国。一条蛇伏在我的胸前，盘绕着我的颈项，从那里吸吮着温热的血液。……
我们向奥博扬斯基道了晚安，一起走上街道。

===Original===
"I have to tell you something; or, rather, I have one question, only one, to put to you." These are my first words.
"I am quite at your service."
From the instant when I begin to speak, the sense of dread passes away from me, and an immense quietude takes its place.
"I must, however, lay down one condition. I will have from you no other answer save the word *Yes* or *No*. I do not wish—and this is of consequence to me—to hear any comments whatever. Do you agree?"
"Most willingly," he returns, with a smile; "the condition that you lay down I certainly shall keep."
"You must know then," I go on, "that, since I became acquainted with you, I have known you for the only man who could make me happy. Some time ago, another man, one who deserves my sympathy and whom I trust, asked me to marry him. Being of opinion that, in the last resort, the knowledge that one is greatly loved may serve as a substitute for happiness, I have taken a month to think the matter over. My decision depends upon your answer. I ought perhaps to add that I can foresee what this is likely to be; but that I am very anxious to get absolute certainty on this point, lest I should at some future time have to reproach myself with having let my chance of happiness go by."
There is a silence.
"May I venture to ask you to put your question in a more definite form?"
"Are you, or are you not, willing to marry me?"
Another silence.
"No: and yet, supposing that. …"
"Remember my condition."
No more is said.
In front of my lodgings we bid each other a calm and friendly farewell.
The next morning, on my way to my office, I put a long scented envelope into a postbox. It is addressed to Janusz.
Nevertheless, the decision which it contains is—not to marry him.
Yes, I am now the bondslave of my soul: these my ice-plains, it is no longer mine to leave them.

===Chinese===
"我必须告诉您一件事；或者更确切地说，我有一个问题——只有一个——要问您。"这是我的第一句话。
"我完全听凭您的安排。"
从我开口说话的那一刻起，恐惧感从我身上消退了，取而代之的是一种巨大的宁静。
"不过，我必须先定一个条件。除了'是'或'不是'，我不要听任何别的回答。我不想——这对我很重要——听任何评论。您同意吗？"
"非常乐意，"他微笑着答道；"您定下的条件，我当然会遵守。"
"那么您要知道，"我继续说，"自从认识您以来，我一直认为您是唯一能让我幸福的男人。不久前，另一个男人——一个值得我信任和同情的男人——向我求婚了。我认为归根结底，知道自己被深深地爱着，可以充当幸福的替代品，于是用了整整一个月来思考这件事。我的决定取决于您的回答。也许我应该补充说，我能够预见这回答可能是什么；但我非常渴望在这个问题上得到绝对的确定，以免将来不得不自责说，我放过了幸福的机会。"
一阵沉默。
"能否请您把问题提得更明确一些？"
"您愿意还是不愿意娶我？"
又是一阵沉默。
"不：不过，假设……"
"记住我的条件。"
不再有任何话语。
在我的住所门前，我们平静而友好地告别了。
第二天早上，在去办公室的路上，我把一个长长的香喷喷的信封投进了邮筒。收信人是雅努什。
然而，信中所包含的决定是——不嫁给他。
是的，我现在是我灵魂的奴仆：这片冰原，我再也不能离开它了。

===Original===
I have done with suffering. … During all these long days and nights, I have not shed one tear. I do not suffer now: the agony-delirium has passed those limits, beyond which no difference is felt between joy and misery, beyond which there is no night of woe, that contrasts with day.
In the still autumn twilight, I am shut up in my dark and lonely room. Lest I should awake my soul, that has fallen asleep, I am pacing the soft carpet with noiseless steps.
I am in terror of the very movements which I myself make. Trembling with cold—or is it with my own emptiness of heart?—and leaning against my doorway in the darkness, wrapped in the folds of my soft shaggy portière, I open my swooning lips to utter a soundless cry, and look staring into the mobile fluttering dark with tired and quiet gaze.
I do not suffer; I exist—in a world wherein the night of woe no longer is a contrast with day, wherein there prevails a tranquil dusk, without sun and without stars.
There is no Ego of mine. I am beyond existence and beyond nothingness—in that world wherein dies the immemorial conflict between dream and vigil; where Wrong, robed in her queenly purple, is no longer shadowed by Vengeance, in her pallid green attire; where stony Hatred no longer hugs in her fierce embrace the weeping god of love; where the marble statue of Pride no longer renders homage to the grim spectre, Fear; wherein there are no more wretched victories, nor the portentous delights of worshipping oneself and the Power of Self!
No, there is no more any Ego of mine. … I am in a world to which even the unlimited fields of Infinity cannot reach, for it was everlastingly beyond all limits. I am in a world in which Duration neither flows nor stands still; wherein Solitude is not, though neither are there spirits to commune with; where there is only no solitude, because there is no Me.
Do I suffer? No. I am in a world where I have no being.
I could well die, if I chose: but my body, well-favoured as it is, would fain not part from my bright, though mournful soul. Therefore am I willing to live.
But there is nought for which I can any longer care; I dwell in a world which my soul is never to behold: for when Death comes, my soul's existence will be over.
Yet not because nothingness is *there*. To believe that *there* is nothingness, one must indeed have an intense power of faith. I cannot bring myself to accept the creed of nothingness. For in the world where I am now, neither Being is, nor Non-Being; there is neither the Ego nor the Non-Ego; nor has the soul ever laid her icy hand upon the body: I am in a world wherein there is no soul of mine.
My soul will end its being at the instant of Death, not because that world is a world of nothingness, but because therein is no such thing as indestructibility of substance.
I might, if I chose, die; but death matters nothing to me. To solve the riddle of life, I do not require death. For now I know all. I know that, in that other world, knowledge and ignorance are not incompatible, nor is there in that world any desire to know. And therefore I shall never solve the riddle of life, because I have solved it now.
I know that which no man knows: that to read the riddle, I need not know all things. For there is no Me!
And I am indeed in a world which contradicts our world, but with a contradiction in which negation and assertion are the same.
But in one thing I do believe—the only thing that is.
And that thing is: *No!*
Such a *No* as does not contradict *Yes*, but means what *No* means, taken together with *Yes*.
Such a *No* as Roslawski said to me.
And if I suffer nothing, it is because I belong to a world wherein joy and sorrow are the same.

===Chinese===
苦难已经结束。……在这些漫长的日日夜夜里，我没有流过一滴泪。我现在不再受苦了：那痛苦的谵妄已经越过了那条界限，在那界限之外，欢乐与痛苦不再有分别，在那界限之外，不再有与白昼相对的悲苦之夜。
在静谧的秋日暮色中，我被关在自己黑暗而孤独的房间里。为了不惊醒我那沉睡的灵魂，我在柔软的地毯上无声地踱步。
我甚至对自己做出的每一个动作都感到恐惧。浑身因寒冷而颤抖——还是因我内心的空虚？——倚靠在黑暗中的门框上，裹在柔软蓬松的门帘褶皱里，我张开昏厥的双唇，发出一声无声的呼喊，以疲倦而平静的目光凝视着那变幻不定的黑暗。
我不再受苦；我存在——在一个悲苦的夜晚不再与白昼对立的世界中，一个只有宁静暮色、没有太阳也没有星辰的世界中。
没有我的自我。我超越了存在与虚无——在一个远古的冲突在梦与醒之间消亡的世界中；在那里，身着紫红王袍的罪恶不再被身穿惨绿衣裳的复仇所笼罩；冷酷的仇恨不再以炽烈的拥抱紧抱哭泣的爱神；骄傲的大理石雕像不再向恐惧的狰狞幽灵俯首致敬；在那里，不再有卑劣的胜利，不再有崇拜自我与自我之力的不祥之乐！
不，不再有我的自我了。……我身在一个甚至无限的广袤也无法企及的世界，因为它永远超越一切界限。我身在一个时间既不流逝也不凝滞的世界；在那里，孤独不存在，尽管也没有可交流的灵魂；在那里之所以没有孤独，是因为没有我。
我受苦吗？不。我身在一个我不复存在的世界。
我完全可以去死——如果我愿意的话：但我这副尚算俊美的躯体，不愿与我那明亮却忧伤的灵魂分离。所以我愿意活着。
然而我已无所牵挂；我居住在一个我灵魂永远无法目睹的世界：因为当死亡来临，我灵魂的存在就将终结。
然而这并非因为那里有虚无。要相信那里有虚无，确实需要一种强大的信仰力量。我无法让自己接受虚无的信条。因为在我此刻所在的世界里，既没有存在，也没有非存在；既没有自我，也没有非自我；灵魂也从未将冰冷的手放在身体之上：我身在一个没有我的灵魂的世界。
我的灵魂将在死亡的瞬间终结其存在，不是因为那个世界是虚无的世界，而是因为那里不存在什么物质不灭。
如果我愿意，我可以去死；但死亡对我毫无意义。要解开生命之谜，我不需要死亡。因为我已经知道了一切。我知道在那个世界里，知识与无知并非不可调和，在那个世界里也没有求知的欲望。因此我永远不会解开生命之谜——因为我现在已经解开了它。
我知道那无人知晓之事：要解开谜底，我不必知道一切。因为没有我！
我确实在一个与我们的世界相矛盾的世界里，但在那矛盾中，否定与肯定是一回事。
但有一件事我确信——唯一存在的那件事。
那件事就是：*不！*
一个不与"是"相矛盾的"不"，它所意味的正是"不"与"是"合在一起时所意味的东西。
一个罗斯拉夫斯基对我说过的"不"。
如果我什么都不再承受，那是因为我属于一个欢乐与悲伤别无二致的世界。

===Original===
On returning from a concert, I went with my friend, Wiazewski, to Lipka, to meet the company we usually see there.

I take some interest in the atmosphere, reeking and tainted though it is, of a high-class restaurant, crowded with "gilded youth," old financiers, beautiful actresses, and demimondaines. The saloon is a large one, lit with wide-branched chandeliers. The air is thick with tobacco-smoke, through which the sparkles of a thousand lights and the brilliant notes of the merry orchestra assail both eye and ear. The ceiling is painted in antique style. The background is all speckled with bright stains\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014blots of white napery on the tables, and candles shaded with glass "lampions" of various tints, forming spots of many a colour. There is a twinkling mingled with a tinkling: the rays of electric blossoms over our heads, and around us the jingling of cups and glasses, join together in a seething tumult.

This is a life apart. Not the daily round of appearances\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014the mere mask which hides life\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014but life immediate, naked, real. You see here that in spite of all it is possible to be merry and to care for nothing. Here are no unsightly garments, no clumsy inartistic motions; no children (that most objectionable element in life!); no "respectable" women, who are to be recognized by their ugliness, their want of style and charm, their tediousness and stupidity, and the fact that, when they think at all, they are always hopelessly depressed. This is a very good illustration of the "Law of Selection": in marriage, the qualities of virtue and fidelity are of more account as guarantees of felicity than such endowments as beauty and health. Beautiful women of a lively temperament are set aside as too knowing, too exacting, and of doubtful trustworthiness: and so they go to swell the ranks of the fallen.

===Chinese===
从一场音乐会回来后，我和朋友维亚泽夫斯基一起去了利普卡，去会合我们常在那儿见到的那群人。

我对一家高级餐厅的氛围颇感兴趣——尽管它充斥着污浊的气息——那里挤满了纨绔子弟、老金融家、美丽的女演员和风尘女子。大厅宽敞，枝形吊灯枝桠横斜，灯火辉煌。空气中弥漫着浓重的烟草雾气，无数灯光的闪烁和欢快乐队的嘹亮音符穿透烟雾，刺激着双眼和双耳。天花板以古典风格绘制。背景尽是斑驳的亮色——桌上洁白餐巾的色块，以及裹着各色玻璃灯笼的烛台所形成的五彩斑点。闪烁与叮当交织在一起：头顶电灯花瓣般的光芒，四周杯盏玻璃的碰撞声，汇成一片沸腾的喧嚣。

这是一种超脱于日常的生活。不是日常表象的周而复始——那仅仅是遮蔽生活的面具——而是直接的、赤裸的、真实的生活。你在这里可以看到，尽管一切如此，人们仍然可以纵情欢乐、无所牵挂。这里没有丑陋的衣裳，没有笨拙而不雅的举止；没有孩子（那是生活中最令人反感的元素！）；也没有体面的女人——她们以丑陋、缺乏风格和魅力、乏味和愚蠢为标志，而且她们一旦思考，总是无可救药地沮丧消沉。这是选择法则的一个极好的注脚：在婚姻中，美德和忠诚作为幸福保障的分量，远大于美貌和健康之类的天赋。性情活泼的美丽女子被排除在外，因为她们过于精明、过于苛求，且值得信赖的程度可疑：于是她们便去壮大了堕落者的队伍。

===Original===
For my own part, did I not fear the accusation of antisocial tendencies, I would, from the height of my cheerless philosophical eminence, declare that I view the "frail sisterhood," as an institution, without intolerance. Therein breathes something that tells of times gone by: something existing, but of which men do not speak. There exist human beings, scorned as a class, whatever their personal endowments may be, with whom no other class is allowed to come in contact, under pain of defilement:\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014not unlike pariahs. These beings are to be bartered for precious metals by means of a secret contract\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014bought as the slaves of ancient times were bought. Their existence is kept a secret quite disinterestedly, for the mere sake of the secret itself: everyone knows all about them. In our days, so hyper-civilized, so deprived of all poetry by reason of excessive culture, this is a most astonishing state of things.

Nearly every man here present has a wife, actual or intended: but these are not permitted to enter: they would be by far too much out of place.

No doubt, their wives, having put the children to bed, had some words with the servant over the daily account of money spent, and put on a clean nightgawn (of a wretchedly bad cut, by the way), say their prayers and lay themselves down to sleep under the red woolen coverlet, thinking all the time: "How late he always returns after these meetings!" or else she may bite her nails with fury, revolving in her mind the idea of another angry scene with her husband\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014a scene foredoomed as heretofore to be without effect. Or again, in agonized resignation, she may bend over the baby\u2019s cradle, and murmur mournfully, with naive pathos: "For your sake, my child!" And the girls whose troths are plighted have long ago gone to sleep under the wing of their domestic guardians, lulled to slumber with some such sweet fancies as: "Most men have intrigues before they marry: he, and he alone, has surely none." And so forth.\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2026

They are foolish\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014but fortunate, because not allowed to come in here.

Ah! once upon a time, in the days of my childish marvellings, how bitterly did I weep over all these things!

===Chinese===
就我个人而言，若非害怕被指控有反社会倾向，我本可以从我那阴郁的哲学高度宣告，我将风尘姐妹作为一种体制来看待，并不怀有偏见。其中弥漫着一种诉说往昔的气息：某种存在着、但人们避而不谈的东西。世间存在着一类人，无论其个人禀赋如何，都被作为一个群体而遭受蔑视，其他任何阶层都被禁止与之接触，否则便有被玷污之嫌——不亚于贱民。这些人可以通过秘密契约被换取贵金属——如同古代的奴隶一样被买卖。她们的存在被极为无私地保守着秘密，纯粹是为了秘密本身：每个人对她们都了如指掌。在我们这个过度文明、因过度文化而丧失了一切诗意的时代，这是一种何等令人惊愕的状况。

在座的几乎每一个男人都有妻子，或法定的、或意定的：但她们不被允许进来：她们会显得太不合时宜了。

毫无疑问，她们的妻子在安顿孩子们就寝之后，为一天的开支账目与佣人争吵了几句，穿上一件干净的睡裙（顺便说一句，剪裁极为糟糕），做了祷告，然后躺在红色羊毛被下入睡，心里一直想着：他每次聚会回来总是这么晚！或者她会愤怒地咬着指甲，在脑海中酝酿着与丈夫的又一次争吵——一场注定一如既往毫无结果的争吵。又或者，在痛苦的隐忍中，她俯身在婴儿的摇篮上，带着天真的伤感哀伤地低语：为了你啊，我的孩子！而那些订了婚的姑娘们早已在她们家庭守护者的羽翼下安然入睡，被诸如此类甜蜜的幻想所催眠：大多数男人在婚前都有过风流韵事：而他，只有他，肯定没有。诸如此类。\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2026

她们是愚蠢的——但也是幸运的，因为不被允许进来。

啊！曾经，在我充满孩童式奇思妙想的日子里，我曾为这一切流过多少苦涩的泪水啊！

===Original===
"Stephen, how late is it?" I asked Wiazewski.

"It will soon be midnight. Our friends are not coming, it would seem. Are you in a hurry to get home?"

"I never am; I have got a latchkey, and so wake nobody when I come in. But are you not yourself sometimes engaged of an evening?"

He shook his head, his teeth shining good-humouredly in a friendly smile.

"You know perfectly well that there is not an assignation I would not set aside to spend an evening with you. To me, friendship is a boon far rarer and far more precious than love."

"I do not hold with you at all. I have enough of the cold consideration granted me by the world."

Stephen smiled again.

"There is no help for it, Janka," he said. "Men of our times are too weakly to love an all-around woman: the very thought of one gives them an unpleasant shock. The day for types of women so extremely complex as you are has now gone by; at present women are preferred who display some very distinct and special characteristic: especially either primitive natures, or such as have been depraved by civilization; or types of spirituality or of sensuality; women either of very well-balanced minds, or nervous even to hysteria; or, again, those in whom warmth of heart or a distinguished bearing prevails. And that is why the monogamic instinct is now dying out completely: in a few years\u2019 time, it will be no more."

Wiazewski was on the warpath, the topic being a favourite one of his.

"For how can a man be true to his wife, if he takes her \u2018for better, for worse,\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2026 till death do them part,\u2019 only, let us say, to kiss a mole that she has on her neck, just under her left ear? Monogamy requires exceedingly strong, rich, abundant natures."

"Then it would follow that our near future would witness our return to the hetairism of primaeval times?"

"No doubt; for both the primitive instincts of the senses, and their ultra-refined activity, have identically the same result."

===Chinese===
斯蒂芬，几点了？我问维亚泽夫斯基。

快到午夜了。看来我们的朋友们不会来了。你急着回家吗？

我从不着急；我有钥匙，进去时不会吵醒任何人。可你自己不也有时候晚上有约吗？

他摇摇头，露出和善的微笑，牙齿在友好的笑容中闪着光。

你完全清楚，没有任何约会是我不能推辞来和你共度一个晚上的。对我来说，友谊是远比爱情更稀有、更珍贵的恩赐。

我完全不同意你的看法。我受够了世人赐予我的那种冷漠的体面。

斯蒂芬又笑了。

没有办法的事，扬卡，他说。我们这个时代的人太软弱，无法去爱一个全方位的女人：光是想到这样的女人就会给他们带来不愉快的震动。像你这样极其复杂的女性类型的时代已经过去了；如今人们偏好那些展现出某种非常鲜明而独特特征的女人：特别是原始的天性，或是被文明败坏了的天性；精神型的或者感官型的；要么是心智极为平衡的女人，要么是神经质到歇斯底里的；又或者是热情洋溢或气度不凡的女人。这正是为什么一夫一妻制的本能如今正在彻底消亡：再过几年，它就将不复存在了。

维亚泽夫斯基此刻斗志昂扬，因为这个话题是他最钟爱的。

一个人怎么能对妻子忠诚呢？假如他娶她时说无论顺境逆境……直到死亡将他们分开，而仅仅——比方说——只是为了亲吻她左耳下方脖子上的那颗痣？一夫一妻制需要极其强大、丰富、充沛的天性。

那是否意味着在不远的将来，我们将回归原始时代的艺妓制度？

毫无疑问；因为无论是感官的原始本能，还是其极度精致的活动，产生的结果完全相同。

===Original===
A handsome woman, with strikingly original features, accompanied by an elderly man, clean-shaven (an actor probably) went by near our table. She too had the look of an actress.

Wiazewski\u2019s eyes followed her with keen scrutiny.

"A fine woman," I remarked.

He turned his eyes away from her.

"She is not my sort," he replied. "Far too cultured for my taste."

Then he again returned to the subject.

"Hetairism, yes. Yes, undoubtedly. But if it all depended upon me, I should wish for one slight restriction.\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2026 You see, one of the most genial types of womanhood is the *wifely* type: that of a woman faithful, trustworthy, absolutely your own.\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2026 It were desirable that such a type should not perish entirely. But I should wish her only as a class to contrast with others, and as a haven of rest, when wearied with those."

I was gazing at the pretty Frenchwoman; suddenly I saw a delighted expression flash over her striking and reposeful face, somewhat harem-like in its beauty. I instinctively followed her glance, and\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014not without somewhat of embarrassed astonishment\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014discovered Imszanski. He was just entering from the doorway, and going through the saloon, distributing on all sides bows or smiles, as a beautiful woman does flowers. His wonderfully sweet and dreamy eyes were seeking someone in the room.

A sudden flash lit them up, as they met the gaze of the handsome Frenchwoman.

Imszanski, on his way to them, happened to see me, and Wiazewski in my company.

Directly, and without showing the least surprise or embarrassment, he changed his expression and saluted us with urbane cordiality, and though he had just gone past our table, he returned, shook hands, and begged leave to sit down beside us.

The Frenchwoman at the neighbouring table was just putting on her gloves, while the actor paid the bill. I should very willingly have told Imszanski not to mind about us, but go on to his acquaintances, who we could see were expecting him. But I refrained, not wishing to lay on his shoulders a burden of gratitude for keeping this matter concealed from Martha, which might later have proved irksome to him.

Stephen, too, understood.

"We are here," he presently said, "waiting for Madame Wildenhoff, Owinski with his intended, and Czolhanski. It is rather late now: I doubt whether they are going to turn up."

===Chinese===
一位容貌美丽的女人从我们桌旁走过，她有着引人注目的独特五官，由一位上了年纪的、剃净胡须的男人陪同（大概是位演员）。她也有一副演员的派头。

维亚泽夫斯基的目光敏锐地追随着她。

好一个美人，我说道。

他将目光从她身上移开。

她不是我喜欢的类型，他回答。太有教养了，不合我的口味。

然后他重新回到这个话题。

艺妓制度，是的。是的，毫无疑问。但如果一切都取决于我，我希望有一个小小的限制。u200bu200bu200bu200bu200bu200bu2026你要知道，女性最可爱的类型之一就是*妻子型*：那种忠诚的、值得信赖的、完全属于你的女人。u200bu200bu200bu200bu200bu200bu2026这样的类型不该完全消亡是件好事。但我只愿她作为一个与其他类型形成对比的阶层而存在，并作为疲惫时歇息的港湾。

我正凝视着那位漂亮的法国女人；忽然，一阵欣喜的表情从她那引人注目而又恬静的脸上掠过，那张脸有着后宫佳丽般的美丽。我不由自主地顺着她的目光望去——在略带窘迫的惊讶中——发现了伊姆尚斯基。他刚从门口进来，穿过大厅，向四面八方点头或微笑，如同一个美女抛洒鲜花一般。他那奇妙的甜美而梦幻般的眼睛正在人群中搜寻某个人。

当他的目光与那位美丽的法国女人相遇时，一道突然的光芒点亮了他的双眼。

伊姆尚斯基在朝她们走去的途中碰巧看到了我和维亚泽夫斯基。

他当即毫无惊讶或窘迫地改变了表情，以温文尔雅的热忱向我们致意。尽管刚才已经走过了我们的桌子，他还是折返回来，握了手，并请求坐在我们旁边。

邻桌的法国女人正在戴手套，那位演员在付账。我很想告诉伊姆尚斯基不必在意我们，去赴他的约会好了——我们看得出那些人在等他。但我忍住了，不想让他肩上多一份因对玛莎隐瞒此事而产生的感激负担，这日后可能会令他厌烦。

斯蒂芬也明白了。

我们在这里，他随即说道，等维尔登霍夫夫人、奥温斯基和他的未婚妻，还有乔尔汉斯基。现在太晚了：我看他们大概不会来了。

===Original===
Imszanski turned aside to say something to a waiter, when he noted with satisfaction that the actors had left the saloon.

He then said he hoped and trusted that we would not look upon him as an intruder, though he had thrust himself on us in such a way.

Czolhanski, a journalist, arrived at about one o\u2019clock, together with Owinski and his fianc\u00e9e, Miss Gina Wartoslawska, whom I had seen several times previously at Imszanski\u2019s.

Her real name is Regina; but she is called Gina. In the movements of her lithe elastic figure is a sort of snakelike suppleness, which tells us of a nervous nature, burning with a passion almost painfully suppressed. She is like a tame panther. Her eyes, long, narrow, partly concealed beneath thin lids, wander hither and thither about the floor with a drooping, apathetic look. Her lips are broad, flattened as it were by many kisses, moist and crimson as if they bled. And, with all that, there is in her something of the type of a priestess.

She came in, drawing black gloves off her slender hands, greeted us with an unsmiling face, and at once called out to a waiter who was passing by:

"A glass of water!"

She drank the whole glass at one draught, and sat down at some distance from the table, with her head bent forward, and her hands clasped over her knees. Owinski took a seat close beside her.

"Czolhanski," he told us, "has only just got through his critique of the leading actress in tonight\u2019s play. We had to stay for him in the editor\u2019s waiting-room."

"Ah," grumbled the critic, "it\u2019s beastly, this work all done to order and at railway speed! Such a piece as that ought to be thought over till it is possible to form a definite judgment upon it. As it is, we are forced to save the situation by means of a lot of sententious generalities."

At last, Madame Wildenhoff arrived with her husband. At the unexpected sight of Imszanski in our company, a deep blush mantled her face. She seated herself next to Gina, and burst into a fit of chuckling, shading her eyes with beautiful hands that carried many a ring. All this was rather unusual and disquieting. Imszanski flushed slightly; a warm haze, so thin that it could scarce be seen, bedimmed his eyes, and his long lashes drooped over them.

Wildenhoff, an unpleasant cut-and-dried sort of man, whose humour inclined to sarcastic silence, proposed that we should pass into a private room. She protested.

"Oh, no! I dearly love noise and music and an uproar all about me. We had better stay here, hadn\u2019t we?"

Wildenhoff smiled at his wife and was presently deep in study of the bill of fare.

She again set to laughing without any cause: a disquieting sort of chuckle, with something like a sob now and then.

I glanced at the two couples, feeling a twinge of envy. "There is love between *them*."\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2026

Oh, but all that was so very, very long ago!

===Chinese===
伊姆尚斯基转身去跟一个侍者交代什么，同时满意地注意到演员们已经离开了大厅。

他说他希望并相信我们不会把他当作不速之客，尽管他以这样的方式硬闯了进来。

乔尔汉斯基是个记者，大约一点钟到达，和奥温斯基以及他的未婚妻吉娜·瓦尔托斯拉夫斯卡小姐一起来的——我以前在伊姆尚斯基家见过她几次。

她的真名是雷吉娜；但大家都叫她吉娜。她柔软而富有弹性的身体运动中有一种蛇一般的柔韧，这告诉我们她有着一种神经质的天性，燃烧着几乎痛苦地压抑着的激情。她像一只驯服的豹。她的双眼狭长，半掩在薄薄的眼睑下，带着一种低垂的、无精打采的目光在地板上游移。她的嘴唇宽大，仿佛被无数个吻压平了，湿润而殷红，像是在渗血。而这一切之中，又有某种女祭司般的气质。

她走进来，从纤细的手上褪下黑色手套，面无表情地向我们致意，随即朝一个路过的侍者喊道：

一杯水！

她一口气喝干了整杯水，然后在离桌子有一段距离的地方坐下来，头向前低着，双手交叉放在膝盖上。奥温斯基紧挨着她坐了下来。

乔尔汉斯基，他告诉我们，刚写完今晚戏剧主演的评论。我们不得不在编辑的接待室等他。

哎，这位评论家抱怨道，这活儿真是糟透了，全都是按订单、以铁路速度赶出来的！那样的作品应该反复思考，直到能形成明确的判断才行。而现在，我们不得不靠一堆故作高深的空话来应付场面。

终于，维尔登霍夫夫人和她的丈夫到了。她出乎意料地在我们中间看到了伊姆尚斯基，脸上立刻涌起了深深的红晕。她在吉娜旁边坐下来，突然爆发出一阵窃笑，用戴着许多戒指的漂亮双手遮住眼睛。这一切相当反常且令人不安。伊姆尚斯基微微涨红了脸；一层极淡的温暖薄雾朦胧了他的双眼，长长的睫毛低垂下来。

维尔登霍夫是个令人不快的刻板男人，性情倾向于嘲讽式的沉默，他提议我们去一个包间。她抗议了。

噢，不！我就是喜欢噪音、音乐和周围一片喧闹。我们最好留在这儿，对吧？

维尔登霍夫微笑着看了看妻子，随即埋头研究起菜单来。

她又毫无缘由地笑了起来：一种令人不安的窃笑，时不时夹杂着近乎啜泣的声音。

我瞥了那两对情侣一眼，感到一阵嫉妒的刺痛。他们之间有爱情。\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2026

噢，可那一切已经过去很久很久了！

===Original===
I wish Stephen would fall in love with me. But he is always running after some theory or other. At times he is as droll as a boarding school girl. I do believe his friendship for me to be absolutely disinterested. He, on his side, declares that a handsome woman, as such, means nothing to him. The type he loves is uncultured, shallow-brained animality.

He is as yet too youthful. Men\u2019s taste for women more spiritualized, more cultured, more quick-witted, is only a reaction: it shows a decline in the vital forces, and tells of old age about to set in.

All the time of our return home, he, rather in the clouds, holds forth with artificial animation.

"With you, Janka, I could well live alone in a wilderness, were you even twice as beautiful as you are\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014and never remember that I was in presence of a being of the other sex. And, indeed, this is the most natural thing in the world: if such a thought ever entered my brain, I should feel humiliated that a woman was mentally my equal."

"But is it with perfect disinterestedness that you have chosen a pretty young woman for your best friend?"

"Why should I not do so? That gives me the advantage of a double pleasure: not only can I enjoy your conversation; I can enjoy your appearance as well."

"You might just as easily take a handsome man for your friend."

"Yes, but then beauty in a woman generally accompanies intelligence; whereas good-looking men are, as a rule, rather foolish. Moreover, however objectively I strive to judge of things, I must confess that a woman\u2019s body is more handsome than a man\u2019s."

"And what of her mind?"

"Why, she has none: I mean there is no such thing as a feminine mind. Though, look you, it is not unlikely that women also have minds. There is nothing sexual about the brain, either way."

"Yet you have always said I had the mind of a man."

"I was wrong; as a friend, you are neither male nor female. You are something that I set in a class apart; and I want you to do the same by me."

At our door, I take leave of the whole company. Imszanski desires to go on with "the ladies" a little farther, but he is back at once. I can guess why.\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2026

===Chinese===
我真希望斯蒂芬会爱上我。但他总是追逐着某种理论或别的什么。有时候他像一个寄宿学校的女生一样滑稽。我确实相信他对我的友谊是完全无私的。而他那一方则宣称，一个美丽的女人，作为女人本身，对他毫无意义。他所喜爱的类型是没有教养的、头脑浅薄的动物性。

他现在还太年轻了。男人对更精神化的、更有文化的、更机敏的女人的品味，不过是一种反作用力：它表明生命力在衰退，预示着老年即将到来。

在我们回家的路上，他始终飘飘然地、做作地兴致勃勃，高谈阔论。

和你在一起，扬卡，即使你比现在美上两倍，我也完全可以独自隐居在荒野之中——而且永远不记得自己身处一个异性的存在面前。而且，这确实是世界上最自然的事了：如果有这样的想法曾进入我的脑海，我会因为一个女人在心智上与我平等而感到屈辱。

但你选择一个漂亮年轻的女人做最好的朋友，这难道完全是出于无私吗？

我为什么不能这样做呢？这样我可以享受双重的愉悦：我既能享受你的谈吐，也能欣赏你的容貌。

你大可以同样轻松地选择一个英俊的男人做朋友。

是的，但女人的美貌通常伴随着智慧；而英俊的男人，一般说来，都相当愚蠢。此外，无论我多么力求客观地判断事物，我必须承认，女人的身体比男人的更美。

那她的心智呢？

嗯，她没有心智：我是说，没有所谓女性心智这种东西。不过，你要知道，女人也可能有心智。大脑在两性之间并无差异。

可你一直说我有一个男人的心智。

我错了；作为朋友，你既不是男性也不是女性。你是我归为一个独特阶层的人；我希望你也这样对待我。

到了家门口，我向所有人告别。伊姆尚斯基想陪女士们再走一段路，但他很快就回来了。我猜得出为什么。\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2026

===Original===
The Imszanskis are, as they have given out, "At Home" on Sundays. From three till dinnertime, the door is practically open to all. These "At Homes" are formal, tedious, and rather pretentious affairs. There are, besides, but few people who come; for Imszanski has no acquaintances with whom he is on really cordial terms.

But I like these Sundays: they soothe my nerves as warm baths do. With the people who come, I need not attempt to keep up any appearance of truthfulness. On the contrary, I say very far-fetched and most fantastic things\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014things, besides, that I know not to be likely to interest anyone present.

But here is the field wherein Imszanski bears away the palm. Never are his movements more elegant, his smiles more cordial, his glances more winning. No one can better than he deal out the small change of social amenities in his looks, his superficial judgments on literature and on art; none, when addressing a compliment to a woman, can more subtly envelope what he means in a mist of allusions.

Both husband and wife appear to advantage. He, with the perfect culture of his ancient and noble descent, is simply enchanting. Martha is a contrast to him, as standing for something newer, but deeper: the culture given by unassuageable sorrow, the concentrated reverie seen in the sad looks of those dark-blue eyes, albeit a kind smile always flutters on her parched red lips.

Now and again, the Wildenhoffs come here on Sundays. They produce a most interesting effect. Everybody is saying that Madame has an intrigue with Imszanski. Martha knows that, and everyone knows that Martha knows: and she feigns ignorance, though aware that no one believes her. So here is being piled up an immense heap of lies: which is a curious situation, and as such not unpleasing to me.

===Chinese===
伊姆尚斯基夫妇如他们所公布的那样，每逢星期天在家会客。从三点到晚餐时间，大门实际上向所有人敞开。这些会客日是正式的、乏味的、相当做作的活动。而且来的人也不多；因为伊姆尚斯基并没有什么与之保持真正亲密关系的熟人。

但我喜欢这些星期天：它们像温水浴一样舒缓我的神经。面对来客，我不必维持任何真诚的表象。恰恰相反，我说些非常牵强和极为荒诞的话——而且，我知道这些话不可能引起在座任何人的兴趣。

但这里是伊姆尚斯基大展身手的舞台。他的举止从未如此优雅，他的微笑从未如此热忱，他的目光从未如此迷人。没有人能比他更出色地在目光中传递社交客套的零钱，在他对文学和艺术的浅薄评判中游刃有余；也没有人能在向女人奉上赞美时，比他更巧妙地将其真实意图裹挟在一层隐喻的薄雾之中。

夫妻二人各展所长。他，凭借其古老高贵血统的完美教养，简直是魅力无穷。玛莎则与他形成对比，代表着某种更新、但更深刻的东西：那无法化解的悲伤所赋予的教养，那些深蓝色眼眸的忧伤凝视中流露的深沉冥想，尽管一个温和的微笑总是在她干裂的红唇上轻轻颤动。

维尔登霍夫夫妇偶尔也在星期天来这里。他们营造出最有趣的效果。人人都在说夫人和伊姆尚斯基有私情。玛莎知道这一点，而且每个人都知道玛莎知道这一点：她却装作不知情，尽管她知道没有人相信她。于是，一座巨大的谎言之山就这样堆砌起来：这是一种奇特的状况，对此我倒也并非不快。

===Original===
Of Madame Wildenhoff, Lombroso would have said that she belonged to the class of courtesans "by right of birth." Her snowy flesh, her golden hair, her brows, blackly looming above azure eyes, her rosy cheeks and scarlet mouth\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014the whole of this fairy colouring gives an appearance of complete artificiality; and her wonderful shape and inborn talent for coquetry make one regret that such gifts should have been lost on such a very unsuitable field of action. For I myself have not the least doubt that need of money is but a secondary motive with those who join the "frail sisterhood." Were it, as is generally supposed, the chief inducement, what should force men to lead lives so similar to the lives of demimondaines?

I like to watch Imszanski with her, playing the part of the host. Nothing, it would seem, nothing in the whole world can possibly throw him off his balance. He greets her just as he would any other visitor, with a set "So-pleased-to-meet-you" sort of smile; gives her as much of his time as he does to any of the women there; and converses with her, partly flirting, partly freezing her with the haughty consciousness of his preeminence as a drawing-room "lion." He makes no endeavour to conceal his liking for her, but shows just as much as it becomes him to have for any young and handsome woman. It would be a breach of the laws of hospitality, if he had not for each of these a few discreet compliments, and for each a look of warm admiration, beaming from those ever half-curtained almond eyes.

Orcio is sometimes called in from the nursery; and in he comes\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014a little fair-haired boy in black velvet, with a superb collar of yellowish lace. The ladies talk to him in French, in order to praise his accent.

Today the following conversation took place:

"*Qui aimes-tu davantage, Georges\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014papa ou maman?*" was the question put to him by Madame Wildenhoff, who, her hand in a white glove of Danish leather, was stroking the boy\u2019s curls with a blandishing smile.

"*C\u2019est papa*," was Orcio\u2019s reply.

"*Et pourquoi donc?*"

"*Parce que maman ne rit jamais.*"

Whereupon everybody set hurriedly to expatiate upon the accomplishments of Orcio\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u200b\u2014who is not yet four! This they did, wishing to hide a certain confusion felt: that enfant terrible had so unconsciously touched on a matter that everyone knew, but no one talked about.

Madame Wildenhoff, who no doubt expected the boy\u2019s answer, and had perhaps elicited it purposely, was the only person to underline its meaning; she let her long eyelashes droop over her rosy cheeks, pretending to be shocked at the unseemly associations that it had by her means called up.

Martha laughed in merry contradiction of what Orcio had just said; then, kissing his fair brow, she told him to make a nice bow to the company and go back to the nursery with the maid.

===Chinese===
关于维尔登霍夫夫人，隆布罗索大概会说她属于凭出身权利即为妓女的那一类。她雪白的肌肤，她金色的头发，她浓黑的眉毛高悬于蔚蓝的双眸之上，她玫瑰色的面颊和猩红的嘴唇——这一切梦幻般的色彩赋予她一种完全人工雕琢的外表；而她美妙的身段和与生俱来的调情天赋，令人惋惜如此禀赋竟被浪费在如此不适宜的舞台上。因为我本人丝毫不怀疑，金钱的需要对于那些加入风尘姐妹行列的人来说只是次要动机。如果它是——如人们普遍以为的那样——主要的诱因，那又有什么力量迫使男人们去过一种与风尘女子如此相似的生活呢？

我喜欢观察伊姆尚斯基与她在一起时扮演主人的样子。似乎没有什么，世界上没有什么能够扰乱他的镇定。他迎接她的方式与迎接其他任何访客别无二致，带着一成不变的幸会幸会式的微笑；他在她身上花费的时间与在其他任何女人身上花费的一样多；和她交谈时，既带着几分调情，又以身为客厅雄狮的优越意识冷落她。他毫不掩饰对她的喜爱，但表现出的也不过是一个男人对任何年轻漂亮女人应有的程度。如果他对每一位这样的女人都没有几句恰到好处的赞美，没有从那总是半掩着的杏仁眼中投去温热的赞赏目光，那便是违反待客之道了。

奥奇奥有时被从育婴室叫进来；他走了进来——一个穿着黑色天鹅绒的小男孩，有着一头金发，戴着一条华丽的淡黄色蕾丝衣领。女士们用法语和他交谈，以便称赞他的口音。

今天发生了如下一段对话：

你更爱谁，乔治——爸爸还是妈妈？维尔登霍夫夫人问道，她的手戴着一只丹麦白皮手套，带着谄媚的微笑抚弄着男孩的卷发。

是爸爸，奥奇奥回答道。

为什么呢？

因为妈妈从来不笑。

于是所有人急忙开始对奥奇奥的才能大加赞赏——他还不满四岁呢！他们这样做，是因为想要掩饰内心的某种尴尬：这个淘气的孩子无意中触及了一个所有人都知道、却没人谈论的话题。

维尔登霍夫夫人无疑预料到了孩子的回答，甚至可能是故意引出来的，她是唯一一个对这番话的含义加以强调的人；她让长长的睫毛低垂在玫瑰色的脸颊上，装出被这些不得体的联想所惊到的样子——正是她本人引出了这些联想。

玛莎愉快地笑着，与奥奇奥刚才所说的话形成鲜明的矛盾；然后，她亲吻了他金色的前额，让他向客人们行个礼，然后跟着保姆回育婴室去。

===Original===
Society is irksome to Martha now. We two often went together formerly to the theatre or to a concert: at present she cares no more to go.

I mostly spend my evenings with her, in interminable conversations. She either relates something to me, or else she "gives sorrow words." I listen.

She is just now much grieved that her husband Witold has for nearly a fortnight hardly ever been at home. Some days we even dine without him.

"It is surely so," she was saying yesterday. "He enjoys his manhood to the full: everything is his. There, he has ‘Bohemian’ society, revelling, fast people, singing, champagne, flowers, and forgetfulness: here, he finds the pure and quiet light of the domestic fireside, the delights of fatherhood, the love of a faithful wife. When he is tired of one sort of pleasure, why then he tries the other.… And *we*—we are all crippled, helpless things—all!"

Silence for a moment.

"There he gets his amusement at the expense of those poor weaklings, whose souls have been wrenched away from them, who have lost the feeling of their human dignity, the consciousness of their right to live, even the very sense of pleasure; who groan under that most unjust burden, their own self-contempt; who feel the continual oppression of a guilt which does not exist, and for whom the first wrinkle is as a sentence of death.

"But on his domestic hearth there beams another fire, and beams on another kind of weakling; a strange creature, now no longer able to descend into Life’s hurly-burly; for whom certain deeds, for many a century regarded with scorn, have through long heredity of atavistic feelings become really loathsome.…

"Our duty is to amuse *them*—the lords of life and death—with the effects of contrast; that *they* may have the assurance of having experienced the whole gamut of emotions, that they may enjoy their manhood to the full."

When Witold came home today from the club (which was at about noon) Martha received him in a beautiful white peignoir, trimmed with Angora fur, and asked him whether he had yet breakfasted. He thanked her graciously, kissed her hand and brow, and desired to see Orcio.

Martha changed colour. She is not so jealous, even of women, as she is of her beautiful little boy, perhaps because he is with her constantly.

The nursemaid brought Orcio, who at once jumped on to his father’s knee, and began talking at the top of his voice about a number of things which had happened to interest him since the day before.

Imszanski was enchanted with the little one, and kissed his rosy face.

For men like him, there is something incomparably sublime and public-spirited in the fact of being a father; this they hold to be the only thing that compensates and atones for the life they lead.

Martha shrank away; standing at a distance, fury in her heart and a smile on her face, she looked on at the father caressing his boy.

"Look, you," she whispered to me, "this—this is my vocation, this the mission of my life; all the pain I have undergone, all the rage of my never-ending and vain revolt, all my disappointed existence; all these have been, only that they two should sit here thus, forgetting me entirely; and that all the wrong done to me by the father should come to life again in that son of his!"

But Witold, having caressed Orcio, went to bed. Not until the evening did he wake up, fresh and hearty-looking, to dine with us, kiss Martha’s hand, retail with lively wit several stories then going the round of the town, and make his way to the club once more.

===Chinese===
社交现在对玛莎来说已经成了负担。我们俩从前常一起去剧院或音乐会：如今她不再有兴趣去了。

我大部分晚上都和她在一起，进行无休无止的交谈。她要么向我讲述些什么，要么"为悲伤赋予言辞"。我倾听着。

她此刻非常痛苦，因为她的丈夫维托尔德已经将近半个月几乎没有在家待过。有些日子我们甚至不等他就用餐了。

"事情确实如此，"她昨天说道。"他尽情享受着他的男性特权：一切都是他的。在那里，他有'波西米亚'式的社交圈，纵情欢宴，放浪形骸，歌唱，香槟，鲜花，忘却一切：在这里，他找到家庭炉火纯净而宁静的光，为人父的快乐，一位忠实妻子的爱。当他厌倦了一种乐趣，他便去尝试另一种。… 而*我们*—我们全都是残废的、无助的东西—全都如此！"

沉默了片刻。

"在那里他以牺牲那些可怜的弱者为代价取乐——那些灵魂被从他们身上撕扯掉的人，那些丧失了人类尊严感、丧失了生存权利意识、甚至丧失了享乐感觉的人；那些在最不公正的重负——他们自己的自我蔑视——之下呻吟的人；那些持续感受到并不存在的罪孽之压迫的人，对他们而言，第一条皱纹就如同死刑判决。

"但在他的家庭炉火旁，另一团火焰照耀着，照耀着另一种弱者；一个奇特的生灵，如今已无法投身于生活的喧嚣之中；对于她，某些在许多世纪里被蔑视的行为，经过返祖情感的长久遗传，已经变得真正令人厌恶了。…

"我们的职责是用对比的效果来取悦*他们*——生命的主宰和死亡的主宰——好让他们确信自己已经历了全部的情感光谱，好让他们充分享受其男性特权。"

今天维托尔德从俱乐部回到家（大约是中午时分）时，玛莎穿着一件饰有安哥拉毛皮的白色精美睡袍迎接了他，问他是否已经用过早餐。他殷勤地感谢了她，亲吻了她的手和前额，并要求见奥奇奥。

玛莎的脸色变了。她对这个漂亮的小男孩的嫉妒甚于对任何女人的嫉妒，也许是因为他一直和她在一起。

保姆带来了奥奇奥，他立刻跳到父亲的膝盖上，开始扯着嗓子讲述从前一天起发生的许多引起他兴趣的事情。

伊姆尚斯基非常喜欢这个小家伙，亲吻了他红润的脸蛋。

对于像他这样的男人来说，为人父这一事实有着无与伦比的崇高和公益精神；他们认为这是唯一能补偿和赎偿他们所过生活的事物。

玛莎退缩了；她站在远处，心中满是愤怒，脸上却挂着微笑，看着父亲疼爱他的孩子。

"你看，"她对我低声说道，"这——这就是我的使命，这就是我生命的全部意义；我所经受的一切痛苦，我无休止而徒劳的反抗中的所有愤怒，我那充满失望的一生——这一切之所以存在，就是为了让他们俩能够这样坐在这里，完全将我遗忘；为了让那个父亲加诸于我的一切不公，都在那个儿子身上重新复活！"

但维托尔德疼爱了奥奇奥之后，便去睡觉了。直到傍晚他才醒来，看上去精神焕发、气色红润，和我们一起用餐，亲吻玛莎的手，以生动的机智讲述几个当时在城里流传的故事，然后再次前往俱乐部。

===Original===
In his love-affairs, Wiazewski is just as fickle and as insatiable as Imszanski; but their "spheres of influence" are different. Wiazewski has a liking for seamstresses, shop-assistants, and so forth; whereas Imszanski is specially interested in cocottes (even his intrigue with Madame Wildenhoff is a case in point). Neither of the two has any great liking for the other, in spite of their mutually courteous bearing at all times. Imszanski has against my friend that he is too democratic: whereas Wiazewski looks on Imszanski as a fool.

The latter explains his dislike for demimondaines thus:

"I have a great liking for misdeeds, but not when committed by professional criminals."

The art of playing with his victims has been brought by him to the acme of perfection. To this end, he employs what naturalists call "mimicry." His features being rather common, he has no trouble in putting a girl off her guard; he makes up as a commercial man, or a lackey, or a waiter; and in such parts he expresses himself most eloquently in the slang of those classes, which he has picked up to perfection.

He is a thorough expert in the art of getting into touch with the minds of such people; and the ease with which he finds his way through a labyrinth of ideas quite unknown to us is truly admirable.

On principle, he is for continual change; but latterly he has been making an exception, and declares he has hit upon the right sort, or nearly so. For some time he has been "keeping company" with a girl, whom he has, on account of her exceptional qualities, distinguished from the common herd. I once saw her at his lodgings and was struck with her good looks.

He has been reading a letter from her today. I asked him to give it to me as a "document," which he very readily consented to do.

It runs thus:

===Chinese===
在情事方面，维亚泽夫斯基和伊姆尚斯基一样反复无常、永不餍足；但他们的"势力范围"不同。维亚泽夫斯基喜欢裁缝女工、女店员之类；而伊姆尚斯基则特别对风尘女子感兴趣（甚至他与维尔登霍夫夫人的私情就是一例）。两人对彼此都没有太多好感，尽管始终保持着彬彬有礼的姿态。伊姆尚斯基对我的朋友不满，认为他太民主了；而维亚泽夫斯基则视伊姆尚斯基为傻瓜。

后者这样解释他对风尘女子的厌恶：

"我非常喜欢恶行，但不喜欢职业罪犯犯下的恶行。"

他玩弄猎物的艺术已经被他臻于完美的境地。为此目的，他运用了博物学家所谓的"拟态"。他的相貌颇为普通，他毫不费力地便能让一个女孩放下戒备；他装扮成一个商人，或者一个仆从，或者一个侍者；在这些角色中，他以这些阶层的行话雄辩地表达自己——那是他完美地学来的。

他是与这类人的心灵建立联系的艺术的真正行家；他在我们完全陌生的思想迷宫中穿行自如的那份从容，确实令人钦佩。

原则上，他主张不断变换；但最近他却破例了，并宣称自己已经找到了合适的人选，或至少差不多了。有一段时间他一直在和一个女孩"交往"，他因为她的出众品质而将她与芸芸众生区别开来。我有一次在他的住所见过她，被她的美貌所打动。

他今天在读她的一封来信。我请他把信给我作为"文献"，他很爽快地答应了。

信的内容如下：

===Original===
> "Dear Stephen I must tell you about something that is Roman the intended husband of Genka came to see me at the shop yesterday evening and he set a-talking to me this way don’t I have no notion where Genka is so I answer back what business of mine is that and he just says don’t you make believe for Genka is in Krucza with that there mechanic and he keeps her I hear is in love with her but I’ll pay him out for it, only the street and the number where he lives are gone clean out of my head can you tell me I know his name is Stephen and I answer this way don’t you go worriting an honest fellow for he don’t have nothing to say to no girls let alone such hussies as Genka he asked me where you lived and I said Krucza number 129 fourth floor and Stephen Tworkowski is your name and he said thankee and hooked it and he says he’ll ask the porter in Wspolna and I said don’t you poke your nose in or you’ll get your head punched as you did once before when you flung dirt at me so if he comes you tell him so and give the beast a talking to.… And something else my dear darling ideal I write this I love you to distraction I am regularly off my head with thinking of you and I have your photo before me and kiss it night and day. O God how I love him more than my life more than my faith I can’t tell what sin I have sinned that I have to pay so dear and you dearest you are so cold and you’ll bring me to my grave with your coldness and in no time too I don’t know but it seems to me you told Elizabeth I slept in Hoza and she makes a mock of me and I don’t care a fig for I am daft for your love no one won’t cure me and no one can’t it’s too late I loved you when I saw you first and shall till my life ends and so long as I don’t put an end to it and who will make me do that but you Stephen my dearest pet and sweetheart.

> "I end this scrawl of mine throwing away my pen crying my eyes out and dying of hunger for that blessed Sunday.

> "Your unhappy or rather lovesick
> Hela."

===Chinese===
> "亲爱的斯蒂芬我必须告诉你一件事就是罗马金卡的未婚夫昨天晚上到店里来找我他就这么跟我说话他说你不知道金卡在哪里吗我就回他说关我什么事他就说你别装了金卡在克鲁恰跟那个机修工在一起他养着她我听说爱上她了但我不会放过他的只是他住的那条街和门牌号我完全想不起来了你能告诉我吗我知道他的名字叫斯蒂芬我就这么回答他说你别去烦一个老实人他跟什么女孩都没关系更别说金卡那种贱货了他问我你住在哪里我就说克鲁恰街129号四楼你的名字叫斯蒂芬他说谢谢就走了他说他会去维斯波尔纳街问门房我说你别多管闲事小心脑袋挨揍就像以前你朝我泼脏水那次那样所以如果他来了你就这么告诉他并且好好教训那个畜生一顿… 还有别的事我亲爱的最理想的人我写这封信我为你疯狂我想你想得都快疯了我把你的照片摆在面前日夜亲吻它天啊我多么爱你胜过我的生命胜过我的信仰我也不知道犯了什么罪要付出这样大的代价而你最亲爱的人你这么冷漠你的冷漠会把我很快送进坟墓我不知道但我好像觉得你告诉伊丽莎白我睡在霍扎街她就笑话我我才不在乎呢我为你痴狂没人能治好我也没人能治好太迟了我第一次见到你就爱上了你并且爱你到生命的尽头只要我自己不结束它谁能让我那样做呢只有你斯蒂芬我最亲爱的宝贝和甜心。

> "我写完这封潦草的信扔下笔泪流满面饿着肚子等着那神圣的星期天到来。

> "你不幸的或者不如说是害了相思病的
> 海拉。"

===Original===
Quite aware that I am doing wrong, I let Martha look back into her past; and I even question her myself so as to bring before her eyes the long dismal perspective of her wounded love, I listen in the manner she likes best, calmly and without any show of compassion. Nor have I any for her, any more than for a fish that must needs live in cold water, or for a bat that cannot bear the sunlight. Martha likes to suffer, and—perhaps for this very reason—she is compelled to suffer. Indeed, she is something of a Sybarite in her almost abnormal sensitiveness to pain. She is fond of telling me all the petty foolish troubles of an injured wife; and this procures her an odd sense of what may be called a sort of enjoyment.

"But, all the same, there was a time once when he loved you, did he not?"

"Oh, Witold declares that up to now he has loved none but me!"

"Well, well; but then at what time did *this*—the present phase begin? For some time at least, he must have been faithful to you."

"Oh, yes, for a few months. Quite at the beginning. Though I myself was never happy.… First of all, during the six weeks before our wedding, I was constantly a prey to such mystic terrors that I came near losing my senses. You know that I do not admit any of those hackneyed maxims of morality—and yet I continually felt that some evil thing was afoot, and a day of reckoning close at hand. And besides, how intolerable then was the thought that now I *had* to marry him, however averse I might feel to the act; that now I had more at stake upon my side than he on his!"

"And afterwards, by the seaside?"

"Oh, then it was entrancing! I almost felt happy. But it lasted so short a time! Shortly after our arrival I fell sick, and grew unwieldy and weakly and plain. And then, if you can believe me, surrounded with all those marvels of nature and of art, I was always longing for Klosow, my own place!"

===Chinese===
我清楚地知道自己在做错事，但我还是让玛莎回顾她的过去；我甚至亲自询问她，以便将她受伤之爱的漫长惨淡前景呈现在她眼前。我以她最喜欢的方式倾听——冷静地，不露丝毫同情。我对她也确实没有同情，正如我对一条只能生活在冷水中的鱼、或一只不能忍受阳光的蝙蝠没有同情一样。玛莎喜欢受苦，而且——也许正因为如此——她被迫受苦。诚然，在她对痛苦近乎异常的敏感中，她有些像一个享乐主义者。她喜欢向我倾诉一个受伤妻子的所有琐碎愚昧的烦恼；这为她带来了一种奇异的享受感。

"可是，不管怎样，他曾经爱过你的，不是吗？"

"哦，维托尔德声称直到如今他只爱过我一个人！"
